(b)

if
r+\tau(r)=0
then for any
\Omega\in(V\otimes V)^{{\mathfrak{g}}}
,

\{\widehat{r}+\lambda\Omega\}_{\lambda\in\mathbb{k}}
is
a family of classical r-matrices for the same Lie cobracket

\delta=\delta_{\widehat{r}}
on
{\mathfrak{t}}
.

Proof.

We need the following

Lemma 6.6
.

If
\Omega\in{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}
is
{\mathfrak{g}}
-invariant
then
\widehat{\Omega}

is
{\mathfrak{t}}
-invariant.

Proof.

Since
f\otimes\id_{{\mathfrak{g}}}
and
\id_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes f
are homomorphisms of
{\mathfrak{g}}
-modules

{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}\to V\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}
and
{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}\to{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes V
, it follows that
\widehat{\Omega}
is
{\mathfrak{g}}
-invariant in
{\mathfrak{t}}\otimes{\mathfrak{t}}
. Let
v\in V
. Then
v=f(x)
for some
x\in{\mathfrak{g}}
. Write
\Omega=\Omega_{1}\otimes\Omega_{2}
in Sweedler-like notation. Then

\displaystyle(\ad v

\displaystyle\otimes\id_{{\mathfrak{g}}}+\id_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes\ad v)(\widehat{\Omega})=(\ad f(x)\otimes\id_{{\mathfrak{g}}}+\id_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes\ad f(x))(f(\Omega_{1})\otimes\Omega_{2}+\Omega_{1}\otimes f(\Omega_{2}))

\displaystyle=[f(x),f(\Omega_{1})]\otimes\Omega_{2}+[f(x),\Omega_{1}]\otimes f(\Omega_{2})+f(\Omega_{1})\otimes[f(x),\Omega_{2}]+\Omega_{1}\otimes[f(x),f(\Omega_{2})]

\displaystyle=(f\otimes f)([x,\Omega_{1}]\otimes\Omega_{2}+\Omega_{1}\otimes[x,\Omega_{2}])=(f\otimes f)(\ad x\otimes\id_{{\mathfrak{g}}}+\id_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes\ad x)(\Omega)=0.\qed

Since
r+\tau(r)
is a
{\mathfrak{g}}
-invariant in
{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}

by Proposition
2.9
(b)
, we have
\widehat{r}+\tau(\widehat{r})=(f\otimes\id_{{\mathfrak{g}}}+\id_{{\mathfrak{g}}}\otimes f)(r+\tau(r))
which is
{\mathfrak{t}}
-invariant by Lemma
6.6
. Note that

\widehat{r}_{i,j}=(f_{i}+f_{j})(r_{i,j})
,
1\leq i<j\leq 3
where

f_{k}:=\id_{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\otimes(k-1)}\otimes f\otimes\id_{{\mathfrak{g}}}^{\otimes(3-k)}
,
k\in\{1,2,3\}
.
Then

\displaystyle\mathbb{\Lbrack}\widehat{r},\widehat{r}\mathbb{\Rbrack}

\displaystyle=[(f_{1}+f_{2})(r_{1,2}),(f_{1}+f_{3})(r_{1,3})]+[(f_{1}+f_{2})(r_{1,2}),(f_{2}+f_{3})(r_{2,3})]

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+[(f_{1}+f_{3})(r_{1,3}),(f_{2}+f_{3})(r_{2,3})]

\displaystyle=[f_{1}(r_{1,2}),f_{1}(r_{1,3})]+[f_{2}(r_{1,2}),f_{2}(r_{2,3})]+[f_{3}(r_{1,3}),f_{3}(r_{2,3})]

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+[f_{1}(r_{1,2}),f_{3}(r_{1,3})]+[f_{2}(r_{1,2}),f_{1}(r_{1,3})]+[f_{2}(r_{1,2}),f_{3}(r_{1,3})]

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+[f_{1}(r_{1,2}),f_{2}(r_{2,3})]+[f_{1}(r_{1,2}),f_{3}(r_{2,3})]+[f_{2}(r_{1,2}),f_{3}(r_{2,3})]

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+[f_{1}(r_{1,3}),f_{2}(r_{2,3})]+[f_{1}(r_{1,3}),f_{3}(r_{2,3})]+[f_{3}(r_{1,3}),f_{2}(r_{2,3})].

Write
r=r_{1}\otimes r_{2}=r^{\prime}_{1}\otimes r^{\prime}_{2}
in Sweedler-like notation. Then,

[f_{1}(r_{1,2}),f_{1}(r_{1,3})]=[f(r_{1}),f(r^{\prime}_{1})]\otimes f_{2}\otimes f^{\prime}_{2}=0,

while

\displaystyle[f_{1}(r_{1,2}),f_{3}(r_{1,3})]=[f(r_{1}),r^{\prime}_{1}]\otimes r_{2}\otimes f(r^{\prime}_{2})=f([r_{1},r^{\prime}_{1}])\otimes r_{2}\otimes f(r^{\prime}_{2})=(f_{1}\circ f_{3})[r_{1,2},r_{1,3}],

since
f
is a homomorphism of
{\mathfrak{g}}
-modules.
Using similar computations for other indices and noting that
f_{i}\circ f_{j}=f_{j}\circ f_{i}
,
i\not=j
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{\Lbrack}\widehat{r},\widehat{r}\mathbb{\Rbrack}=(f_{1}\circ f_{3}+f_{1}\circ f_{2}+f_{2}\circ f_{3})(\mathbb{\Lbrack}r,r\mathbb{\Rbrack})=0.

Then part
(a)
follows from Proposition
2.9
.

To prove part
(b)
, we need the following immediate

Lemma 6.7
.

If
\Omega\in V\otimes V
is
{\mathfrak{g}}
-invariant then
\Omega
is also
{\mathfrak{t}}
-invariant. In particular,
if
\Omega\in{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}
is
{\mathfrak{g}}
-invariant then
(f\otimes f)(\Omega)
is
{\mathfrak{t}}
-invariant.

By Lemma
6.7
,

\delta_{\widehat{r}}=\delta_{\widehat{r}+\lambda\Omega}
for all
\lambda\in\mathbb{k}
. Thus, it remains to prove that
\mathbb{\Lbrack}\widehat{r}+\lambda\Omega,\widehat{r}+\lambda\Omega\mathbb{\Rbrack}=0
. Since
\Omega\in(V\otimes V)^{{\mathfrak{t}}}
by Lemma
6.7
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{\Lbrack}\widehat{r}+\lambda\Omega,\widehat{r}+\lambda\Omega\mathbb{\Rbrack}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{\Lbrack}\widehat{r},\widehat{r}\mathbb{\Rbrack}+\lambda\mathbb{\Lbrack}\widehat{r},\Omega\mathbb{\Rbrack}+\lambda\mathbb{\Lbrack}\Omega,\widehat{r}\mathbb{\Rbrack}+\lambda^{2}\mathbb{\Lbrack}\Omega,\Omega\mathbb{\Rbrack}

\displaystyle=\lambda[(\id_{{\mathfrak{t}}}\otimes\Delta)(\widehat{r}),\Omega_{1,3}+\Omega_{2,3}]+\lambda[\Omega_{1,2}+\Omega_{1,3},(\Delta\otimes\id_{{\mathfrak{t}}})(\widehat{r})]

\displaystyle=\lambda[\widehat{r}_{1,2}+\widehat{r}_{1,3},\Omega_{1,3}]+\lambda[\Omega_{1,3},\widehat{r}_{1,3}+\widehat{r}_{2,3}]

\displaystyle=\lambda[\widehat{r}_{1,2}-\widehat{r}_{2,3},\Omega_{1,3}]=\lambda\tau_{2,3}([\widehat{r}_{1,3}-\widehat{r}_{3,2},\Omega_{1,2}]).

Since
\tau(r)=-r
,
\tau(\widehat{r})=-\widehat{r}
and, therefore,

\mathbb{\Lbrack}\widehat{r}+\lambda\Omega,\widehat{r}+\lambda\Omega\mathbb{\Rbrack}=\lambda\tau_{2,3}([(\Delta\otimes\id_{{\mathfrak{t}}})(\widehat{r}),\Omega_{1,2}]=0.\qed

Now, let
\mathbf{c}:I_{n}\to\mathbb{k}
be transitive, let
\boldsymbol{\alpha}:[n]\to\mathbb{k}^{n}
, let
\Omega\in(V\otimes V)^{{\mathfrak{g}}}
and
let
r

be a skew-symmetric classical r-matrix for
{\mathfrak{g}}
. By Theorem
4.4
,

\mathbf{r}(\mathbf{c},\boldsymbol{\alpha})=\sum_{i,j\in[n]}\widehat{r}_{i,j+n}+\sum_{i\in[n]}\boldsymbol{\alpha}(i)\Omega_{i,i+n}+\sum_{1\leq i<j\leq n}\mathbf{c}(i,j)(\Omega_{j,i+n}-\Omega_{j+n,i})

is a classical r-matrix for
{\mathfrak{t}}^{\oplus n}
. In particular, if

\tau(\Omega)=\Omega
then

\mathbf{r}(\mathbf{c},\boldsymbol{\alpha})=\sum_{i,j\in[n]}(\widehat{r}_{i,j+n}+\mathbf{c}(i,j)\Omega_{i,j+n}),

where
\mathbf{c}
is extended to a map
[n]\times[n]\to\mathbb{k}
via
\mathbf{c}(i,i)=\boldsymbol{\alpha}(i)
,
i\in[n]
and
\mathbf{c}(j,i)=-\mathbf{c}(i,j)
,
(i,j)\in I_{n}
.

6.3.
Poisson structures for functions on Takiff groups

Let
G
be an algebraic group whose Lie algebra is
{\mathfrak{g}}
and
consider
\mathbb{k}[V\rtimes G]\cong\mathbb{k}[V]\otimes\mathbb{k}[G]

as an algebra. Furthermore,
\mathbb{k}[V]
is generated by
V^{*}
as an algebra. Note that the group
G
acts on
\mathbb{k}[V]
on the right by algebra automorphisms
via

(\xi\triangleleft g)(v)=\xi(gv),\qquad\xi\in V^{*},\,g\in G,\,v\in V,

and acts naturally on
\mathbb{k}[G]
both on the left and on the right.

Fix a basis
B
of
V

and a dual basis
\{\xi^{b}\}_{b\in B}
of
V^{*}
. Then
V^{*}\otimes V
is naturally a
coalgebra via
\Delta(\xi\otimes v)=\xi\otimes\sum_{b\in B}b\otimes\xi^{b}\otimes v
,
v\in V
,
\xi\in V^{*}
, which, clearly,
is independent of the choice of
B
.

Lemma 6.8
.

The assignments
\xi\otimes v\mapsto\rho_{\xi,v}
where
\rho_{\xi,v}(g)=\xi(gv)
,
\xi\in V^{*}
,
v\in V
,
g\in G
, define a homomorphism
of coalgebras
\rho:V^{*}\otimes V\to\mathbb{k}[G]
. In particular,

\Delta(\rho_{\xi,v})=\sum_{b\in B}\rho_{\xi,b}\otimes\rho_{\xi^{b},v}
,
\xi\in V^{*}
,

v\in V
.

Proof.

Since
u=\sum_{b\in B}\xi^{b}(u)b
for all
u\in V
,
we have, for all
v\in V
,
\xi\in V^{*}
and
g,g^{\prime}\in G
,

\displaystyle(((\rho\otimes\rho)\circ\Delta)(

\displaystyle\xi\otimes v))(g\otimes g^{\prime})=\Big(\sum_{b\in B}\rho_{\xi,b}\otimes\rho_{\xi^{b},v}\Big)(g\otimes g^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\sum_{b\in B}\xi(gb)\xi^{b}(g^{\prime}v)=\xi(gg^{\prime}v)=\rho_{\xi,v}(gg^{\prime})=\Delta(\rho_{\xi,v})(g\otimes g^{\prime}).\qed

Lemma 6.9
.

The natural left and right actions of
V\rtimes G

on
\mathbb{k}[V]\otimes\mathbb{k}[G]
are defined by

\displaystyle v\triangleright\xi=\xi+\rho_{\xi,v},

\displaystyle\xi\triangleleft v=\xi(v)+\xi,

for all
v\in V
,
\xi\in V^{*}
, together
with the trivial left
G
-action on
\mathbb{k}[V]
,
natural right
G
-action on
\mathbb{k}[V]
,
natural left and right actions of
G
on
\mathbb{k}[G]

and trivial left and right actions of
V
on
\mathbb{k}[G]
.

Proof.

Let
\xi\in V^{*}
. We have, for all
v,v^{\prime},v^{\prime\prime}\in V

and
g,g^{\prime},g^{\prime\prime}\in G

\displaystyle((v,g)

\displaystyle\triangleright((v^{\prime},g^{\prime})\triangleright\xi))(v^{\prime\prime},g^{\prime\prime})=(v,g)\triangleright(\xi+\rho_{\xi,v^{\prime}})(v^{\prime\prime},g^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\xi(v^{\prime\prime})+\rho_{\xi,v}(g^{\prime\prime})+(g\triangleright\rho_{\xi,v^{\prime}})(g^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\xi(v^{\prime\prime})+\xi(g^{\prime\prime}v)+\rho_{\xi,v^{\prime}}(g^{\prime\prime}g)=\xi(v^{\prime\prime})+\xi(g^{\prime\prime}v)+\xi(g^{\prime\prime}gv^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\xi(v^{\prime\prime})+\rho_{\xi,v}(g^{\prime\prime})+\rho_{\xi,gv^{\prime}}(g^{\prime\prime})=((v+gv^{\prime},gg^{\prime})\triangleright\xi)(v^{\prime\prime},g^{\prime\prime}).

Similarly,

\displaystyle((\xi\triangleleft(v,g))

\displaystyle\triangleleft(v^{\prime},g^{\prime}))(v^{\prime\prime},g^{\prime\prime})=((\xi(v)+\xi\triangleleft g)\triangleleft(v^{\prime},g^{\prime}))(v^{\prime\prime},g^{\prime\prime})

\displaystyle=\xi(v)+(\xi\triangleleft g)(v^{\prime})+((\xi\triangleleft g)\triangleleft g^{\prime})(v^{\prime\prime})=\xi(v)+\xi(gv^{\prime})+\xi(gg^{\prime}v^{\prime\prime}),

while
(\xi\triangleleft(v+gv^{\prime},gg^{\prime}))(v^{\prime\prime},g^{\prime\prime})=\xi(v+gv^{\prime})+\xi(gg^{\prime}v^{\prime\prime})
.
All remaining cases are obvious.
∎

Corollary 6.10
.

The natural right and left action of

{\mathfrak{t}}=V\rtimes{\mathfrak{g}}
on
\mathbb{k}[V]\otimes\mathbb{k}[G]
is determined by
\mathbb{k}[G]
-linear derivations

\partial_{v},{}_{v}\partial
of
\mathbb{k}[V]\otimes\mathbb{k}[G]

defined by

\partial_{v}(\xi)=\xi(v),\qquad{}_{v}\partial(\xi)=\rho_{\xi,v},\qquad v\in V,\,\xi\in V^{*}

together with the zero left action of
{\mathfrak{g}}
on
\mathbb{k}[V]
, the natural right action of
{\mathfrak{g}}

on
\mathbb{k}[V]
, zero left and right actions of
V
on
\mathbb{k}[G]
and natural left and right actions of
{\mathfrak{g}}

on
\mathbb{k}[G]
given by (
2.13
).

Now, suppose that
{\mathfrak{g}}
is quasi-triangular with an r-matrix
r=r_{1}\otimes r_{2}
in Sweedler-like notation and let
f:{\mathfrak{g}}\to V
be a surjective homomorphism of
{\mathfrak{g}}
-modules. The Poisson
bracket on
\mathbb{k}[V\rtimes G]
induced by
\widehat{r}\in{\mathfrak{t}}\otimes{\mathfrak{t}}
is given by

\displaystyle\{\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}\}=0,

\displaystyle\{\xi,\varphi\}=\xi(f(r_{1}))(\varphi\triangleleft r_{2})-\rho_{\xi,f(r_{1})}\cdot(r_{2}\triangleright\varphi),

\displaystyle\{\xi,\xi^{\prime}\}=\xi(f(r_{1}))(\xi^{\prime}\triangleleft r_{2})+\xi^{\prime}(f(r_{1}))(\xi\triangleleft r_{2}),

for all
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in V^{*}
,
\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{k}[G]
. Thus,
\mathbb{k}[G]

is a Poisson-commutative Poisson ideal in
\mathbb{k}[V\rtimes G]
and
\mathbb{k}[V]

is its Poisson subalgebra. Note that the Poisson
bracket
\{\xi,\xi^{\prime}\}
,
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in V^{*}
is just the Lie bracket
on
V^{*}
induced by the Lie coalgebra structure
on
{\mathfrak{t}}
.

Let
\Omega=\Omega_{1}\otimes\Omega_{2}\in V\otimes V
in Sweedler-like notation be

{\mathfrak{g}}
-invariant.
Then
\Omega\bowtie(\varphi\otimes\varphi^{\prime})=0=\Omega\bowtie(\xi\otimes\varphi)
,

\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{k}[G]
,
\xi\in V^{*}
while
for all
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in V^{*}

\Omega\bowtie(\xi\otimes\xi^{\prime})=(\xi\otimes\xi^{\prime})(\Omega)-\rho_{\xi,\Omega_{1}}\otimes\rho_{\xi^{\prime},\Omega_{2}}.

Assume for simplicity that
\tau(\Omega)=\Omega
and let
\mathbf{c}:I_{n}\to\mathbb{k}
be transitive. Extend
\mathbf{c}

to a map
[n]\times[n]\to\mathbb{k}
by
\mathbf{c}(j,i)=-\mathbf{c}(i,j)
,
i,j\in[n]
.
Using the notation from §
4.5
and (
4.9
) we obtain

\displaystyle\{\varphi^{(k)},\varphi^{\prime}{}^{(l)}\}_{\mathbf{c}}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\{\xi^{(k)},\varphi^{(l)}\}_{\mathbf{c}}

\displaystyle=\xi(f(r_{1}))(\varphi\triangleleft r_{2})^{(l)}-\rho_{\xi,f(r_{1})}{}^{(k)}\cdot(r_{2}\triangleright\varphi)^{(l)},

\displaystyle\{\xi^{(k)},\xi^{\prime}{}^{(l)}\}_{\mathbf{c}}

\displaystyle=\xi(f(r_{1}))(\xi^{\prime}\triangleleft r_{2})^{(l)}+\xi^{\prime}(f(r_{1}))(\xi\triangleleft r_{2})^{(k)}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathbf{c}(k,l)(\xi(\Omega_{1})\xi^{\prime}(\Omega_{2})-\rho_{\xi,\Omega_{1}}{}^{(k)}\cdot\rho_{\xi^{\prime},\Omega_{2}}{}^{(l)}),

for all
k,l\in[n]
,
\xi,\xi^{\prime}\in V^{*}
and
\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}\in\mathbb{k}[G]
. Note that
\mathbb{k}[G]^{\otimes n}

is still a Poisson-commutative Poisson ideal of
\mathbb{k}[V\rtimes G]^{\otimes n}

but
\mathbb{k}[V]^{\otimes n}
is no longer a Poisson subalgebra.

We will now provide more explicit calculations for
G=GL_{m}
,
{\mathfrak{g}}={\mathfrak{gl}}_{m}
and an isomorphism
f:{\mathfrak{g}}_{\ad}\to V
of
{\mathfrak{g}}
-modules. Denote
by
x_{i,j}\in\mathbb{k}[G]
the usual matrix element function (cf. §
6.1
) and define

\bar{x}_{i,j}\in\mathbb{k}[G]
by
\bar{x}_{i,j}(g)=x_{i,j}(g^{-1})
,
g\in G
. Then
\mathbb{k}[G]
identifies with the localization of
\mathbb{k}[x_{i,j}\,:\,i,j\in[m]]

by
\det
.
It is easy to check that

\sum_{k\in[m]}x_{i,k}\bar{x}_{k,j}=\delta_{i,j}=\sum_{k\in[m]}\bar{x}_{i,k}x_{k,j}
and
\Delta(\bar{x}_{i,j})=\sum_{k\in[m]}\bar{x}_{k,j}\otimes\bar{x}_{i,k}
,
i,j\in[m]
.

Let
\{y_{i,j}\}_{i,j\in[m]}

be the basis of
V^{*}
dual to
\{f(E_{i,j})\}_{i,j\in[m]}
.
We claim that

\rho_{y_{i,j},f(E_{a,b})}=x_{i,a}\bar{x}_{b,j},\qquad i,j,a,b\in[m].

Indeed, it is easy to check that the assignment
y_{i,j}\otimes f(E_{a,b})\mapsto x_{i,a}\bar{x}_{b,j}
,
i,j,a,b\in[m]
define a homomorphism of coalgebras
V^{*}\otimes V\to\mathbb{k}[G]
.
Since
G
is generated by invertible diagonal matrices and by the
\exp(tE_{r,s})
,
t\in\mathbb{k}
,
r\not=s
, it is enough to verify that
y_{i,j}(f(gE_{a,b}g^{-1}))=x_{i,a}(g)\bar{x}_{b,j}(g)=x_{i,a}(g)x_{b,j}(g^{-1})
for any
g\in G
in one of these forms. If
g=\sum_{r\in[m]}\lambda_{r}E_{r,r}
with
\lambda_{r}\in\mathbb{k}^{\times}
,

r\in[m]
, we have

y_{i,j}(f(gE_{a,b}g^{-1}))=\sum_{r,s}y_{i,j}(\lambda_{r}\lambda_{s}^{-1}E_{r,r}E_{a,b}E_{s,s})=\lambda_{a}\lambda_{b}^{-1}\delta_{i,a}\delta_{j,b}=x_{i,a}(g)x_{b,j}(g^{-1}),

while for
g=\exp(tE_{r,s})=1+tE_{r,s}
,
r\not=s\in[m]
,
t\in\mathbb{k}
,

\displaystyle y_{i,j}(f(gE_{a,b}g^{-1}))

\displaystyle=y_{i,j}(f((1+tE_{r,s})E_{a,b}(1-tE_{r,s})))

\displaystyle=\delta_{i,a}\delta_{j,b}+t\delta_{s,a}\delta_{i,r}\delta_{j,b}-t\delta_{i,a}\delta_{j,s}\delta_{r,b}-t^{2}\delta_{s,a}\delta_{r,b}\delta_{i,r}\delta_{j,s}

\displaystyle=(\delta_{i,a}+t\delta_{i,r}\delta_{s,a})(\delta_{j,b}-t\delta_{j,s}\delta_{r,b})

\displaystyle=x_{i,a}(1+tE_{r,s})x_{b,j}(1-tE_{r,s})=x_{i,a}(g)x_{b,j}(g^{-1}).

Summarizing, we have for all
i,j,a,b\in[m]
.

f(E_{a,b})\triangleright y_{i,j}=x_{i,a}\bar{x}_{b,j},\quad y_{i,j}\triangleleft f(E_{a,b})=\delta_{i,a}\delta_{j,b},\quad E_{a,b}\triangleright y_{i,j}=0,\,\,y_{i,j}\triangleleft E_{a,b}=\delta_{i,a}y_{b,j}-\delta_{b,j}y_{i,a}.

Let
\Omega=\sum_{a,b\in[m]}E_{a,b}\otimes E_{b,a}
be the canonical
{\mathfrak{g}}
-invariant in
{\mathfrak{g}}\otimes{\mathfrak{g}}
. Then

\displaystyle(f\otimes f)(\Omega)

\displaystyle\bowtie(y_{i,j}\otimes y_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}})=\delta_{i,j^{\prime}}\delta_{i^{\prime},j}(1\otimes 1)-\sum_{a,b\in[m]}x_{i,a}\bar{x}_{b,j}\otimes x_{i^{\prime},b}\bar{x}_{a,j^{\prime}}.

Note that applying the multiplication map to this tensor yields
0
, as expected.

To write explicit Poisson brackets, we now need to choose a skew-symmetric r-matrix. For instance, by
[
20
]
*Example 5.2,

(6.4)

r=\sum_{t\in[m]}d_{t}(E_{1,t}\otimes E_{t,m}-E_{t,m}\otimes E_{1,t}),

where
d_{t}=2-\delta_{t,1}-\delta_{t,m}
,
t\in[m]
, is a skew-symmetric
classical r-matrix for
{\mathfrak{gl}}_{n}
; in particular, the well-known unique skew-symmetric
solution of CYBE for
{\mathfrak{gl}}_{2}
belongs to this family.
We have, for all
i,j,i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\in[m]

\displaystyle\widehat{r}\bowtie

\displaystyle(y_{i,j}\otimes x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}})=\sum_{t\in[m]}d_{t}\Big(y_{i,j}\triangleleft f(E_{1,t})\otimes x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\triangleleft E_{t,m}-y_{i,j}\triangleleft f(E_{t,m})\otimes x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\triangleleft E_{1,t}

\displaystyle\phantom{(y_{i,j}\otimes x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}})=\sum_{t\in[m]}d_{t}}-f(E_{1,t})\triangleright y_{i,j}\otimes E_{t,m}\triangleright x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}+f(E_{t,m})\triangleright y_{i,j}\otimes E_{1,t}\triangleright x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\Big)

\displaystyle=d_{j}\delta_{1,i}\delta_{i^{\prime},j}\otimes x_{m,j^{\prime}}-d_{i}\delta_{m,j}\delta_{1,i^{\prime}}\otimes x_{i,j^{\prime}}+d_{j^{\prime}}x_{i,j^{\prime}}\bar{x}_{m,j}\otimes x_{i^{\prime},1}-\delta_{j^{\prime},m}x_{i,1}\sum_{t\in[m]}d_{t}\bar{x}_{t,j}\otimes x_{i^{\prime},t},

\displaystyle\widehat{r}\bowtie

\displaystyle(y_{i,j}\otimes y_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}})=\sum_{t\in[m]}d_{t}\Big(y_{i,j}\triangleleft f(E_{1,t})\otimes y_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\triangleleft E_{t,m}-y_{i,j}\triangleleft f(E_{t,m})\otimes y_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\triangleleft E_{1,t}

\displaystyle\phantom{(y_{i,j}\otimes y_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}})=\sum_{t\in[m]}d_{t}}+y_{i,j}\triangleleft E_{1,t}\otimes y_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\triangleleft f(E_{t,m})-y_{i,j}\triangleleft E_{t,m}\otimes y_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\triangleleft f(E_{1,t})\Big)

\displaystyle=d_{j}\delta_{i,1}\otimes(\delta_{i^{\prime},j}y_{m,j^{\prime}}-\delta_{m,j^{\prime}}y_{i^{\prime},j})-d_{i}\delta_{j,m}\otimes(\delta_{1,i^{\prime}}y_{i,j^{\prime}}-\delta_{i,j^{\prime}}y_{i^{\prime},1})

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+d_{i^{\prime}}(\delta_{1,i}y_{i^{\prime},j}-\delta_{i^{\prime},j}y_{i,1})\otimes\delta_{j^{\prime},m}-d_{j^{\prime}}(\delta_{i,j^{\prime}}y_{m,j}-\delta_{j,m}y_{i,j^{\prime}})\otimes\delta_{i^{\prime},1},

while
\widehat{r}\bowtie(x_{i,j}\otimes x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}})=0
.
This yields the following Poisson bracket on
\mathbb{k}[V\rtimes G]

\displaystyle\{x_{i,j},x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle=0,

\displaystyle\{y_{i,j},x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle=d_{j}\delta_{i^{\prime},j}(\delta_{1,i}x_{m,j^{\prime}}-\delta_{j^{\prime},m}x_{i,1})+d_{i}\delta_{m,j}\delta_{1,i^{\prime}}x_{i,j^{\prime}}+d_{j^{\prime}}x_{i,j^{\prime}}\bar{x}_{m,j}x_{i^{\prime},1},

\displaystyle\{y_{i,j},y_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}\}

\displaystyle=(\delta_{1,i}+\delta_{i,m}-\delta_{1,j^{\prime}}-\delta_{j^{\prime},m})\delta_{1,i^{\prime}}\delta_{j,m}y_{i,j^{\prime}}-(\delta_{1,i^{\prime}}+\delta_{i^{\prime},m}-\delta_{1,j}-\delta_{j,m})\delta_{1,i}\delta_{j^{\prime},m}y_{i^{\prime},j}

(6.5)

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\delta_{i,j^{\prime}}d_{i}(\delta_{j,m}y_{i^{\prime},1}-\delta_{i^{\prime},1}y_{m,j})-\delta_{i^{\prime},j}d_{i^{\prime}}(\delta_{j^{\prime},m}y_{i,1}-\delta_{i,1}y_{m,j^{\prime}})

for
i,j,i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\in[m]
. The last bracket,
as already mentioned, is the Lie bracket on
V^{*}
induced by the Lie coalgebra structure on
{\mathfrak{t}}
.
Then for
n\geq 2
and
transitive
\mathbf{c}:I_{n}\to\mathbb{k}
extended
to a skew-symmetric
\mathbf{c}:[n]\times[n]\to\mathbb{k}

we get the following Poisson bracket
on
\mathbb{k}[V\rtimes G]^{\otimes n}

\displaystyle\{y_{i,j}^{(k)},y_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}^{(l)}\}_{\mathbf{c}}

\displaystyle=\delta_{1,i}\delta_{j^{\prime},m}(d_{i^{\prime}}y_{i^{\prime},j}^{(k)}-d_{j}y^{(l)}_{i^{\prime},j})-\delta_{1,i^{\prime}}\delta_{j,m}(d_{i}y_{i,j^{\prime}}^{(l)}-d_{j^{\prime}}y_{i,j^{\prime}}^{(k)})

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\delta_{i,j^{\prime}}d_{i}(\delta_{j,m}y_{i^{\prime},1}^{(l)}-\delta_{1,i^{\prime}}y_{m,j}^{(k)})-\delta_{i^{\prime},j}d_{i^{\prime}}(\delta_{j^{\prime},m}y^{(k)}_{i,1}-\delta_{1,i}y^{(l)}_{m,j^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\phantom{=}+\mathbf{c}(k,l)\Big(\delta_{i,j^{\prime}}\delta_{i^{\prime},j}-\sum_{a,b\in[m]}x_{i,a}^{(k)}\bar{x}_{b,j}^{(k)}x_{i^{\prime},b}^{(l)}\bar{x}_{a,j^{\prime}}^{(l)}\Big),

\displaystyle\{y_{i,j}^{(k)},x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}^{(l)}\}_{\mathbf{c}}

\displaystyle=d_{j}\delta_{1,i}\delta_{i^{\prime},j}x^{(l)}_{m,j^{\prime}}-d_{i}\delta_{m,j}\delta_{1,i^{\prime}}x^{(l)}_{i,j^{\prime}}+d_{j^{\prime}}x^{(k)}_{i,j^{\prime}}\bar{x}_{m,j}^{(k)}x^{(l)}_{i^{\prime},1}-\delta_{j^{\prime},m}x_{i,1}^{(k)}\sum_{t\in[m]}d_{t}x^{(l)}_{i^{\prime},t}\bar{x}^{(k)}_{t,j},

(6.6)

\displaystyle\{x_{i,j}^{(k)},x_{i^{\prime},j^{\prime}}^{(l)}\}_{\mathbf{c}}

\displaystyle=0,\mskip 200.0mui,j,i^{\prime},j^{\prime}\in[m],\,k,l\in[n].

6.4.
Small quantum algebra at roots of unity

Following
[
26
]
, we describe here an example of a family of
R-matrices for the small quantum group at roots of unity corresponding
to
{\mathfrak{sl}}_{2}
. Let
\mathbb{k}=\mathbb{C}
and let
q\in\mathbb{k}^{\times}
be a primitive root of unity of order
2\ell>2
.
The algebra
H=u_{{\mathfrak{q}}}({\mathfrak{sl}}_{2})

is generated by
E
,
F
and
L^{\pm 1}
subject to relations

[E,F]=\frac{L^{2}-L^{-2}}{q-q^{-1}},\quad LEL^{-1}=qE,\quad LFL^{-1}=q^{-1}F,\quad E^{\ell}=F^{\ell}=0,\quad L^{2\ell}=1.

This is a Hopf algebra of dimension
4\ell^{3}
with the comultiplication

\Delta(E)=E\otimes L^{2}+1\otimes E,\quad\Delta(F)=F\otimes 1+L^{-2}\otimes F,

L^{\pm 1}
being group-like. By
[
26
]
, we obtain a
family of R-matrices parametrized by
\{1,-1\}^{2}
for the same comultiplication, namely

\displaystyle R^{(\epsilon,1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq\ell-1\\
0\leq i,j\leq 4\ell-1\end{subarray}}\,q^{\binom{k}{2}-\frac{1}{2}ij}\epsilon^{ij}\,\frac{(q-q^{-1})^{k}}{[k]_{q}!}L^{i}E^{k}\otimes L^{j}F^{k},

\displaystyle R^{(\epsilon,-1)}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{4\ell}\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}0\leq k\leq\ell-1\\
0\leq i,j\leq 4\ell-1\end{subarray}}\,q^{-\binom{k}{2}+\frac{1}{2}ij}\epsilon^{ij}\,\frac{(q^{-1}-q)^{k}}{[k]_{q}!}F^{k}L^{i}\otimes E^{k}L^{j}

where
\epsilon\in\{1,-1\}
and
[k]_{q}!=\prod_{1\leq t\leq k}(q^{t}-q^{-t})/(q-q^{-1})
; here
R^{(\epsilon,-\epsilon^{\prime})}=\tau(R^{(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime})})^{-1}
,
(\epsilon,\epsilon^{\prime})\in\{1,-1\}
.

We will now use this example to show that Drinfeld twists produced by our construction
are not, generally speaking, equivalent. Let
R=R^{(1,1)}
and consider two Drinfeld twists corresponding to the identity permutation and its counterpart corresponding to
(1,2)
, that is

J=R_{2,3}
,
J^{\prime}=R_{3,2}^{-1}
.
We claim that
J^{\prime}
cannot be obtained from
J
the same way the classical
twist corresponding to the transposition
(1,2)
is obtained from the classical twist corresponding
to the identity permutation, that is, by conjugating with the permutation of factors (cf. Remark
4.8
).
For, it is sufficient to show that

(\tau_{1,2}\otimes\tau_{1,2})(J)J^{\prime-1}=\tau_{1,2}\tau_{3,4}(R_{2,3})R_{3,2}=R_{1,4}R_{3,2}

does not commute with the action of
H^{\otimes 2}

given by the standard comultiplication

\Delta_{H^{\otimes 2}}

on the 4th tensor power of some
H
-module
V
.
Indeed, suppose that
\ell>2
and let
V
be the 3-dimensional
H
-module with the
basis
v_{i}
,
0\leq i\leq 2
and the action given by

Fv_{i}=v_{i+1}
,
i\in\{0,1\}
,
Fv_{2}=0
,
Ev_{0}=0
,
Ev_{i}=(q+q^{-1})v_{i-1}
,

i\in\{1,2\}
and
Lv_{i}=q^{1-i}v_{i}
,
0\leq i\leq 2
. Note that

\frac{1}{4\ell}\sum_{0\leq i,j\leq 4\ell-1}q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}ij}L^{i}\otimes L^{j}(v_{r}\otimes v_{s})=\Big(\frac{1}{4\ell}\sum_{0\leq i\leq 4\ell-1}q^{i(1-r)}\sum_{0\leq j\leq 4\ell-1}q^{(1-s\pm\frac{i}{2})j}\Big)v_{r}\otimes v_{s}.

The inner sum is equal to
0
unless
1-s\pm\frac{1}{2}i\equiv 0\pmod{2l}
which
happens if and only if
i=\mp 2(1-s)
. Thus,

\frac{1}{4\ell}\sum_{0\leq i,j\leq 4\ell-1}q^{\pm\frac{1}{2}ij}L^{i}\otimes L^{j}(v_{r}\otimes v_{s})=q^{\mp 2(1-r)(1-s)}v_{r}\otimes v_{s}.

Let
u=v_{2}\otimes v_{1}\otimes v_{2}^{\otimes 2}
. Since
\Delta_{H^{\otimes 2}}(F)=F\otimes 1^{\otimes 3}+L^{-2}\otimes 1\otimes F\otimes 1
,

\Delta_{H^{\otimes 2}}(F)(u)=0
.
On the other hand,

\displaystyle R_{1,4}R_{3,2}(u)

\displaystyle=R_{1,4}(u+(q^{2}-q^{-2})v_{2}\otimes v_{2}\otimes v_{1}\otimes v_{2})=q^{2}u+(q^{4}-1)v_{2}\otimes v_{2}\otimes v_{1}\otimes v_{2}.

Therefore,
\Delta_{H^{\otimes 2}}(F)R_{1,4}R_{3,2}(u)=q^{2}(q^{4}-1)v_{2}^{\otimes 4}\not=0

since we assumed that
2\ell>4
.

More generally, one obtains families of
R
matrices in
extensions of the small quantum group at a root of unity by group like
elements. Such an extension depends on a pair of weight lattices
\Lambda^{\prime}\subset\Lambda
, and
R-matrices are parametrized by certain pairings between subgroups of
\Lambda/\Lambda^{\prime}

with values in
\mathbb{k}^{\times}

(see
[
26
]
*Theorem A).
